# Milestone 4: experiments E1-E4

Generated by `scripts/m4_report.py` from `results/models` (1000 bootstrap replicates, seed 0; the reference models of the main campaign (`scripts/m2.py`) in E1 and E3); definitions in `docs/experiments.md`, section 3. Tables: 3 decimals, `n/a` = not available. Per-window tables are used only when they reproduce their JSON summary (decision M4-5: in milestone 3 the transfer evaluation overwrote the own `p1.<h>.parquet`).

## Campaigns

| campaign | expected | found | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| main | 75 | 75 | complete |
| seeds | 150 | 150 | complete |
| paired | 160 | 160 | complete |
| event | 16 | 16 | complete |
| transfer (training) | 36 | 36 | complete |
| transfer (P1 files) | 378 | 288 | partial |

Not complete: `transfer (P1 files)` (partial). Tables that need them are marked *not available* below. `transfer (P1 files)` counts the `p1_<other>.json` of every label over the six datasets.

## E1: one-step error relative to persistence (H5.1)

`ratio` = RMSE_a(model) / RMSE_a(persistence) of P0 on fold 0 of the driver split (seed 0); `low`, `high`: 95 % interval from 1000 driver-bootstrap replicates with common random numbers (the follower counts are drawn once per dataset and applied to every table). `seed_min`/`seed_max`: range over training seeds 0-2; `folds_min`/`folds_max`: range over the clean arms of folds 0-4 (probe events, SUBSET only); n/a with fewer than two runs.

## E1b: closed-loop spacing error from 1 s windows to the whole event

`<h>_rmse_s`: pooled spacing RMSE of P1 at the horizon (1 s windows from protocol `p1s`, 10 s and 30 s from `p1`, the whole event); `<h>_median_rmse_s`: median over the windows (over the events for the whole event); `whole_top5_share`: share of the whole-event summed squared spacing error carried by the five followers with the largest sums. The taus between the P0 ranking and the horizon rankings are point estimates (intervals of the P1 horizons: E3).

## E2: leakage inflation (H5.2)

Paired design (decision R2): both arms scored on the same probe events of folds 0-4, pooled; `inflation` = (clean - leaky) / clean (positive: the leak lowers the error); `mean_difference` (clean - leaky), its interval and `p_bootstrap` from `paired_difference` (1000 driver replicates, common random numbers), `p_wilcoxon` the signed-rank test on the per-driver differences, `p_holm` the Holm adjustment of `p_bootstrap` over the models of a dataset, subset and metric, `p_holm_wilcoxon` that of `p_wilcoxon` over the same family. Subset `all`: every probe event; `affected`: the probe events of drivers with at least one event in the leaky training part of the same fold.

### ngsim

Folds with runs: 0, 1, 2, 3, 4; models: 8 of 8.
Affected probe events: 3561 of 11405 (31.2%) for ddpg, drivers 3127.

## E3: rankings across protocols (H5.3, H5.4)

Point values of fold 0, seed 0 (lower is better; P2 and the synthetic P4 over the profiles and scenarios inside the speed range of the training data; P1 whole and P0 from `train.json`). Kendall's tau-b between every pair of rankings: `tau` and `p` on the point values, `low`/`high` the 95 % interval and `tau_boot` the mean over 1000 replicates (drivers for P0, P1 and the empirical P4; in-range profiles for P2 and the composite, speeds with an equilibrium for P3, in-range scenarios for the synthetic P4). A model without a value ranks last.

## E3b: composite variants and sensitivity

The composite of E3 (`v1`: mean normalised rank of the concave-growth error, the hysteresis error, the unstable fraction and the 100-vehicle collisions) has no realism input: a follower that keeps a large gap and never reacts is stable and never collides. The variants add the whole-event P1 spacing RMSE (`v1+p1`), the relative fundamental-diagram error of P2 (`fd_error_rel`; `v1+fd`) or both (`v2`, six inputs, equal weights). `borda v2` is the Borda count of v2 (sum of n - rank, normalised to [0, 1] with 0 = best); with average ranks on ties it is an affine function of the rank sum, so it equals v2 by construction. Leave one out: v2 without each of its inputs; weights: v2 with weight 2 on the unstable fraction, on the collisions and on both (the stability and collision inputs). `fd_error_rel` is pooled over the samples of every P2 profile (the 20 OpenACC windows and the pulse: P2 has no in-range version), compares the density bins with at least 200 samples on both sides (`n_FD_bins`, which differs between models), and has no per-profile version to resample: it is held at its point value in the replicates. Intervals (1000 replicates, seed 0): the rank interval of v2 and the interval of tau(P0, composite) resample the in-range profiles and the speeds with an equilibrium, as in E3, and the drivers of the whole-event P1 input with the draw of P0 (common random numbers); the v1 row reproduces the P0-composite row of E3. Ranks: 1 = best, average ranks on ties. `top3 changes`: entries into and exits from the top three (ties at the third position counted in) against v1 for the variants and against v2 for Borda, leave one out and weights. Pareto view: `on front` marks the models that no other model matches or beats in both the whole-event P1 error and composite v2 (both lower is better; the P1 error is one of the six inputs of v2).

## E3c: training ablations of the networks

Campaign `ablation` (decision M4-7): rollout-loss horizons of 15 s and 30 s (`*_loop_h15`, `*_loop_h30`), a 3 s input history (`*_hist30`), a four-fold width (`*_wide`) and the stability penalty evaluated in a neighbourhood of every equilibrium (`stability_mlp_nbhd`); fold 0, seed 0 of the driver split, same training budget as the reference entries. `unstable` = numerical P3 fraction at 0.05 m/s, `large` at 1 m/s; collisions of P2 over the in-range profiles, P4 synthetic in range. The 15 s and 30 s horizons do not exist on FollowNet-highD (15 s events).

## E3d: the composite with the human platoon reference (protocol p2h)

P2 scores the platoons against recorded followers behind the OpenACC leaders (17 of 19 windows with ACC followers); p2h repeats the protocol behind human-driven chains of NGSIM and TGSIM and the two manual AstaZero runs (docs/protocols.md). `acc_*`: in-range P2 inputs, `human_*`: the same inputs of p2h; `composite_v1h` and `composite_v2h`: the variants v1 and v2 built from the human reference (P1 whole-event error and P3 unstable fraction unchanged). Taus are point estimates between the rankings of the models of the main campaign.

## E3e: rank agreement inside the model families

Kendall's tau-b between the P0 ranking and the composite, the P3 unstable fraction and the whole-event P1 spacing error over all entries, inside the learned entries (`network`) and inside the mechanistic entries, per dataset; point estimates with the two-sided p-value of scipy's kendalltau (the mechanistic family has six entries, so no bootstrap interval is given). The anti-correlation of E3 is partly between the two families, whose one-step and traffic-level merits are opposite, and partly inside the learned family.

## E3f: robustness of tau(P0, composite) (exploratory)

Added after the results (2026-11-16); none of these checks was registered in advance. All taus are Kendall's tau-b over a fixed set of entries of the main campaign (the 25 reference entries, or the subset named), fold 0 and seed 0 of the driver split unless stated otherwise. One call of `e3_replicates` per dataset (1000 replicates, seed 0) gives the replicates of P0, the whole-event P1 error and the four composite inputs, shared by every treatment below.

### Missing-input rule and set of entries

`last`: the rule of the composite (a missing input ranks last among the set); `skip`: every input is ranked among the entries of the set that have it and an entry is averaged over the inputs it has. Sets: all 25 entries, without the two baselines (23), and the entries with all four inputs (`complete`; the inputs missing are P3 unstable fractions, listed per dataset). Replicate r recomputes the composite with the treatment from the r-th replicates of the inputs of the set. `low`/`high`: 95 % percentile interval; `share_below`: share of replicate taus strictly below the point tau; `BC`: bias-corrected percentile interval (z0 from that share). The `published` rows reproduce tau, low and high of the P0-composite rows of E3 (largest absolute difference 0.0e+00).

### Collision gate

An entry is admissible when its share of colliding followers in the in-range P2 profiles, `collisions_100 / (100 n)` with n = `n_profiles_in_range` of `p2.json` (the synthetic braking pulse included, as in the collision input), is at most theta (no collision value: inadmissible). Gated score = composite (`last` rule on the set) + 1 for an inadmissible entry: every admissible entry ranks before every inadmissible one and the composite orders each group. The gate is re-evaluated in every replicate from the replicate collisions (the denominator stays 100 n).

### The collision input

The collision input counts the followers whose own gap closes (`followers`); a collided vehicle keeps driving. Followers that do not react to their leader (persistence, constant speed) therefore count one collision per profile, that of the first follower, and the input rewards the absence of a reaction. Two replacements from `p2.vehicles.parquet` (in-range profiles, 100 followers): `blocked`, the followers at or behind the first colliding one, and `profiles`, the profiles with at least one collision. tau(P0, composite) with the input replaced (`last` rule), over all entries and without the baselines; the profile draws of the replicates are those of E3 (the `followers` rows reproduce the published composite).

### The stability verdict

The protocol calls an equilibrium unstable when the maximal gain exceeds 1 + 1e-5 (`published`). A model that passes a perturbation on unchanged (a pure time shift such as Newell's: gain one at every frequency, 0.9995 at 0.1 Hz in the discrete simulation) is stable under this rule. Two other rules from the saved gains of `p3.speeds.parquet`: `tolerance 1e-3` (maximal gain above 1 + 1e-3) and `neutral counts as unstable` (unstable under the published rule, or a gain at 0.1 Hz above 1 - 0.001). tau(P0, composite) with the unstable fraction recomputed under each rule; `changed`: entries whose unstable fraction differs from the published one. The speed draws of the replicates are those of E3.

| dataset | set | rule | n_models | n_changed | changed | tau | p | 95% CI | top3 |

### Statistics of the registered tests in the other training runs (exploratory; the verdicts are those of fold 0, seed 0)

Point values, no intervals, for every run of the seed and fold check on the main datasets and, on `highd`, the run under the split by blocks of recordings (fold 0, seed 0; decision HD-5). These runs cannot change the verdicts. Entries: those with `train.json`, `p1.json`, `p2.json` and `p3.json` of that run (`missing`: the others). `tau`, `top3`: tau(P0, composite `last`) and the composite top three over all of them. H5.3: `p0_top3` and `whole_top3`, the three most accurate entries by the one-step error and by the whole-event closed-loop spacing error, and `h53_dropped_*`, how many of them are not in the composite top three. H5.1: over the `h51_n` entries that are not baselines, the ratio of the P0 error (`train.json`) to that of persistence in the same run: `h51_within10` (0.9 <= ratio <= 1.1), `h51_beyond30` (ratio < 0.7 or > 1.3; split into `h51_better30` and `h51_worse30`), and the smallest ratio. H5.4: tau between the rankings by spacing and by speed error of P1 at 10 s, 30 s and over the whole event (a missing value last). Every column: `e3f_run_statistics.csv`.

### Registered rule H5.3

"By the composite at least 2 of the 3 best models by trajectory RMSE drop out of the top three; refuted when the top threes coincide." `dropped`: entries of the trajectory top three (P0 or whole-event P1) not in the composite top three (`last` rule on the set; ties broken by model name); `p_boot_ge2` and `p_boot_coincide`: shares of replicates with dropped >= 2 and dropped = 0.

## E3g: one-step accuracy and the composite by training objective (exploratory)

Added after the results (2026-11-16). The families of E3e differ in what their entries are fitted to: the mechanistic entries are calibrated in closed loop on spacing and speed and never on the one-step acceleration, most learned entries are trained on the one-step error. The entries are grouped by objective (`trafficloop/experiments/within_family.py`, `OBJECTIVES`; the column "training or calibration" of the entries table). Ranks are average ranks among the reference entries of the dataset (1 = best).
